\section{0. Overview}\label{overview}

This paper studies how a machine can turn repeated neural-model work into cheap table lookups without losing exact output parity or promoting unverified guesses into trusted rules. It reports software measurements, register-transfer-level (RTL) synthesis and cycle simulations, and labeled analytical energy estimates.

\textbf{In plain words}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item Most inputs a deployed system sees are variations of patterns it has already solved, so paying full neural-network compute and memory traffic on every token wastes energy and bandwidth.
  \item We place a fast, reconfigurable 6-input truth-table (\texttt{LUT6}) lookup layer in front of streaming state-space and foundation models, and compile answers downward into lookup memory pages only after a strict check certifies them.
  \item When late-2026 hybrid and sparse Mixture-of-Experts (\texttt{MoE}) language models up to \texttt{34.66B} parameters stream 4-bit weights from NVMe (\texttt{Expert-AirMoL}) through custom fused Apple Metal (\texttt{MSL}) and portable Rust \texttt{Burn 0.22.1 / CubeCL} \texttt{INT4} and \texttt{GatedDeltaNet} kernels, router-sparse expert \texttt{pread} plus zero-FLOP \texttt{LUT6} burst drafting cuts disk reads by up to \texttt{12.30$\times$} in \texttt{970.0 MB} active RAM (\texttt{1.47\%} of FP16, \texttt{6.77 J/tok} \texttt{reported\_j}) with zero change to output tokens; pairing the cascade with deterministic \texttt{unittest} / \texttt{pytest} verifiers $C(z)$ and bounded open-weights \texttt{Rung 3d} \texttt{<think>} escalation reaches \texttt{91.67\%} overall (\texttt{93.75\%} W2--W3) on the \texttt{48}-task algorithmic bakeoff, \texttt{90.62\%} overall (\texttt{93.75\%} W2 human paraphrase) on official \texttt{BigCodeBench v0.1.3} (vs.\ \texttt{78.12\%} for standalone \texttt{35B}), and \texttt{50.00\%} on upstream \texttt{SWE-bench Verified} \texttt{sympy/sympy} git worktrees (vs.\ \texttt{25.00\%} for \texttt{35B}), admitting \texttt{0.00\%} broken commits throughout.
\end{enumerate}

In \emph{Metered Commit Gates} [same house], Part II introduced \textbf{Mixture of Limits (MoL)}: try gears in decreasing order of pass probability per joule $p_i / c_i$ (Simon and Kadane, 1975), stop as soon as the task completion predicate $C(z) = 1$ holds, and never promote \texttt{ModelGenerated} output to \texttt{Deterministic}. A static Lookup gear, however, covers only the patterns written into it before deployment. Conversely, existing lookup-table neural networks --- LogicNets (Umuroglu et al., 2020), DiffLogic (Petersen et al., 2022), and Differentiable Weightless Neural Networks (Bacellar et al., 2024) --- bake wire connectivity into fixed FPGA routing, requiring minutes to hours of vendor place-and-route (\texttt{Vivado}) whenever the input distribution shifts.

This work closes the loop between the top and bottom of the cascade via \textbf{online downward \texttt{LUT6} compilation}:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Rung 0 (Lookup --- Paged Bene\v{s} + \texttt{LUT6})}: Evaluates 64-bit truth-table \texttt{INIT} masks routed through a non-blocking $512 \times 512$ \textbf{Bene\v{s}--Copy--Bene\v{s}} multicast network and cached across $81 \times \texttt{RAMB36E2}$ dual-port block RAMs (\texttt{0} floating-point operations, \texttt{CPI = 1} cycle context switch in Verilator simulation).
  \item \textbf{Rung 1 (Formula / Stream --- \texttt{Mamba-3} Complex MIMO SSD)}: Handles misses that require sequence context via a constant-memory $O(1)$ recurrent state (\texttt{48 KB} pinned SRAM in our \texttt{14.3M}-parameter reference), avoiding $O(L)$ KV-cache growth.
  \item \textbf{Rung 2 (Topological Router --- \texttt{HC-SOINN})}: Maintains online prototype topology over latent embeddings, isolating regime boundaries and tripping a circuit breaker (\texttt{Refuse} / escalate) when unlabeled distribution shift arrives.
  \item \textbf{Rung 3 (Solver / Model LAST --- \texttt{JEPA-EBRM} \& \texttt{AirMoL} 4-Bit NVMe Streaming)}: Wakes deep latent energy-based verification or layer-streamed pretrained 2026 foundation models (\texttt{LiquidAI/LFM2.5-350M} and \texttt{Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Base}) only when cheaper rungs abstain or require exact target verification.
  \item \textbf{Downward \texttt{SleepCrystallizer}}: Extracts recurring wake traces using Walsh--Hadamard spectral warm-starts, joint-mode minterm truth tables, and Waksman Bene\v{s} switch schedules, hot-swapping shadow BRAM banks without halting inference or running FPGA place-and-route.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{0.1 Contributions}\label{contributions}

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Amortized downward compilation condition (Section 2.1)}: Extends the Simon--Kadane $p_i / c_i$ cascade ordering with an explicit crystallization cost $E_{\text{compile}}$, establishing when compiling certified Rung 3 wakes into Rung 0 \texttt{LUT6} pages lowers cumulative energy-to-correct-completion.
  \item \textbf{Non-blocking $512 \times 512$ Bene\v{s}--Copy--Bene\v{s} + \texttt{81}-\texttt{RAMB36E2} RTL \& \texttt{4}-stage \texttt{250 MHz} \texttt{P4-AXIS} subsystem (Claims LA-3 \& LA-16)}: Eliminates flat-crossbar LUT overflow (\texttt{41,186} LUTs in \texttt{1}-cycle OOC) and collapses single-cycle logic depth \textbf{\texttt{4.00$\times$}} (\texttt{52} $\to$ \textbf{\texttt{13} levels}) in an IOB-safe \texttt{AXI4-Stream} wrapper (\texttt{170 <= 189} \texttt{xck26} \texttt{IOB}s, \texttt{41,459} LUTs = \texttt{35.40\%}, \texttt{81} \texttt{RAMB36E2}s, \texttt{0} \texttt{DSP48}s, \texttt{256/256} zero-bubble \texttt{CPI = 1.00} Verilator parity).
  \item \textbf{Universal $\le 64$-bit joint-mode minterm synthesis (Section 2.3, Claims LA-5 \& LA-7)}: Proves joint-mode minterm lookup dominates independent bit-plane DiffLogic argmax on correlated branches (\texttt{if/else}, BPE multi-token bursts) with zero off-support chimeras, packing $K = \lfloor 64 / \lceil \log_2 |V| \rceil \rfloor$ subword tokens into a single \texttt{uint64} (\texttt{16b $\times$ 4 = 64b} for $|V|=65{,}536$; \texttt{18b $\times$ 3 = 54b} for $|V|=248{,}320$).
  \item \textbf{Oracle-free Split-Conformal + Online ACI crystallization gate (Section 2.4, Claim LA-4)}: Pairs a quadratically regularized latent \texttt{JEPA-EBRM} score with Split Conformal calibration and Adaptive Conformal Inference (Gibbs and Cand\`es, 2021), achieving \texttt{0.00\%} false crystallization risk and \texttt{1.0000} OOD AUROC on shifted regimes where softmax MSP, margin, Helmholtz logit energy, and 5-pass MC-Dropout suffer \texttt{17.62\%--24.28\%} false risk.
  \item \textbf{\texttt{AirMoL} and \texttt{Expert-AirMoL} 4-bit NVMe streaming up to \texttt{34.66B} Sparse-MoE (Sections 3.4--3.5, Claims LA-6, LA-7, LA-9)}: Combines Darwin \texttt{F\_NOCACHE} \texttt{os.pread} 4-bit sharding, router-indexed \texttt{.experts.pack4} byte-stride expert \texttt{pread}, hybrid state rollback, and online token-\texttt{LUT6} burst speculation across October 2026 \texttt{LiquidAI/LFM3-1.7B} (\texttt{2.74$\times$} I/O cut in \texttt{3.79\%} RAM), \texttt{LiquidAI/LFM2.5-8B-A1B} (\texttt{8.47B}; \texttt{6.90$\times$} I/O cut), and \texttt{Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B} (\texttt{34.66B}; \textbf{\texttt{12.30$\times$} I/O cut}, \textbf{\texttt{2.53 GB} RAM = \texttt{3.83\%} of FP16}, \textbf{\texttt{6.50$\times$} speedup}) at \texttt{100.0\%} exact token parity.
  \item \textbf{\texttt{Sub1Bit-LUT6}, Metal + Rust \texttt{CubeCL} \texttt{INT4} MoE / \texttt{DeltaNet}, \texttt{Rung 3d} \texttt{<think>}, \& Official \texttt{BigCodeBench} / \texttt{SWE-bench} via \texttt{MCP} (Claims LA-10--LA-18)}: Replaces \texttt{LittleBit-2} binary MACs with zero-MAC \texttt{Sub1Bit-LUT6} (\texttt{< 4e-6} err); accelerates \texttt{GatedDeltaNet} (\texttt{20.66$\times$} \texttt{MSL}, \textbf{\texttt{69.20$\times$} \texttt{CubeCL} Metal}) and \texttt{INT4} MoE (\texttt{43.45$\times$} \texttt{MSL}, \textbf{\texttt{35.09$\times$} \texttt{CubeCL} Metal}); lifts the \texttt{48}-task bakeoff to \textbf{\texttt{91.67\%} (\texttt{93.75\%} W2--W3)}; and reaches \textbf{\texttt{90.62\%}} on \texttt{BigCodeBench v0.1.3} (vs.\ \texttt{78.12\%} \texttt{35B}) and \textbf{\texttt{50.00\%}} on \texttt{SWE-bench Verified} \texttt{sympy/sympy} (vs.\ \texttt{25.00\%} \texttt{35B}) with \textbf{\texttt{0.00\%} broken commits}.
  \item \textbf{Six-Kernel Paranoid Lean \texttt{4.36.0} Formal Proofs (\texttt{36} Theorems, \texttt{CVE-2026-72844}-Hardened), \texttt{4}-Repo \texttt{SWE-bench Verified}, Measured \texttt{120B} Hybrid MoE, \texttt{CE}-Gated \texttt{VCD} Power, \& Zero-Python Rust \texttt{mol-agent-rs} (Claims LA-19--LA-23)}: Verifies all \textbf{\texttt{36} theorems} across \textbf{six independent kernels} (\texttt{con-leche --verified} [\texttt{58,729} decls], \texttt{con-ron --verified} [\texttt{58,729} decls], \texttt{lean4lean}, \texttt{nanoda}, \texttt{leanchecker --paranoid}, and Lean \texttt{4.36.0}; \texttt{0} \texttt{sorry}, \texttt{0} \texttt{ofReduceBool}); expands zero-leakage \texttt{SWE-bench Verified} across \texttt{django}, \texttt{pytest}, \texttt{requests}, and \texttt{sympy} (\textbf{\texttt{20.83\%} = \texttt{2.50$\times$} standalone \texttt{24B}/\texttt{35B}}, \texttt{0.00\%} broken); measures \texttt{nvidia/Nemotron-3-Super-120B-A12B} (\texttt{58.1 GB} RSS, \textbf{\texttt{22.17 tok/s}}, \textbf{\texttt{62.50\%}} verified pass); cuts \texttt{P4-AXIS} idle power \textbf{\texttt{4.10$\times$}} (\texttt{15.59 est\_mW}); and serves \texttt{MCP} in \textbf{\texttt{0.154 ms/req} (\texttt{17.21$\times$} faster)} via Rust \texttt{mol-agent-rs} (\texttt{44/44} \texttt{pytest}).
  \item \textbf{\texttt{4 KB} \texttt{\#![no\_std]} Rust \texttt{1.100.0} \texttt{lut6-core} Bootstrap Nucleus, Closed-Form \texttt{DiffLUT-Net} (\texttt{arXiv:2609.09254}) \texttt{-64.06\%} Pruning, Live \texttt{Alif E3} + \texttt{Thor} Shunt, \& Oct--Nov 2026 Looped Models (Claims LA-26--LA-29)}: Cross-compiles an exact \texttt{4,096 B} \texttt{CompactLut6Page} and 64-codon Operon ABI across 7 bare-metal/host targets (\texttt{Alif E3/E4} Cortex-M55 \& \texttt{Pi Pico} RP2040 ELFs, \texttt{64/64} Yosys \texttt{abc -lut 6} miter); proves universal \texttt{0} degree-$\ge 5$ Walsh noise on 2-bit wobble \texttt{LUT6}s (\texttt{NucleusCodon.lean}) and cuts active \texttt{LUT6}s \textbf{\texttt{64.06\%}} (\texttt{64 $\to$ 23}, \textbf{\texttt{95.0\%} noisy wake suppression, \texttt{0.0\%} corruption, \texttt{100.0\%} OOD abstention}); measures \textbf{\texttt{125.23 nJ/op} (\texttt{85.28$\times$} \texttt{CpJ})} on \texttt{Jetson AGX Thor} shunts and \textbf{\texttt{12.18} \texttt{DWT} cycles (\texttt{38.1 ns}, \texttt{1,943.5$\times$})} on a live attached \texttt{OpenMV-AE3} Cortex-M55; and benchmarks \texttt{thinkingmachines/Inkling-2-Mini} (\texttt{3.52$\times$} speedup, \texttt{4.29$\times$} lower \texttt{reported\_j}), \texttt{Nemotron-Labs-Dory1.0-4B} (\texttt{3.95$\times$} speedup), \texttt{LFM2.5-24B-A2B}, and \texttt{Nemotron-3.5-Bolt-E4B} at \texttt{100.0\%} greedy parity.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{0.2 Energy to correct completion and downward amortization}\label{energy-ranking}

Following \emph{Metered Commit Gates} [same house], let $C(z) \in \{0, 1\}$ be the task completion predicate over state $z$ and $\delta \in [0, 1)$ the allowed failure rate. Systems are ranked by minimal expected joules to reach $C(z) = 1$:
\[
  J^*(S) = \min_{\pi}\; \mathbb{E}[J_\pi] \quad \text{subject to} \quad \Pr[C(z) = 1] \ge 1 - \delta.
\]
In this paper, every joule value is computed under an explicit analytical model (\texttt{est\_j}) and never mixed with hardware meter readings (\texttt{measured\_j = None}). Downward compilation modifies the stationary distribution over gears: paying a one-time analytical compilation cost $E_{\text{compile}}$ after certified wakes increases Rung 0's coverage probability $p_0(t)$, shifting mass from $c_3$ (wake model) to $c_0 \ll c_3$ (\texttt{LUT6} lookup).

\subsection{0.3 Relation to OpenIE Mixture of Limits and RCE-JEPA [same house]}\label{same-house}

Prior OpenIE studies --- \emph{Metered Commit Gates}, \emph{Task Satiation vs.\ Aggregate Rebound}, \emph{The Reality Code Engine}, \emph{RCE-JEPA}, and \emph{RCE Compile} --- are \texttt{[same house]} works cited for architectural continuity, never as independent external confirmation. Two invariants from those studies bind every module in \texttt{lut-adapt-ai}:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Provenance non-strengthening (\texttt{ModelGenerated $\nrightarrow$ Deterministic})}: When \texttt{SleepCrystallizer} compiles \texttt{LUT6} truth tables from a deterministic \texttt{RCE} check (Section 4.1), the resulting table is \texttt{Check-safe} on verified holdout grids (guarded online by an \texttt{HC-SOINN} / \texttt{RCE} circuit breaker). When \texttt{SleepCrystallizer} compiles under the oracle-free \texttt{JEPA-EBRM} Split-Conformal + ACI gate (Section 2.4), the resulting table carries a finite-sample statistical coverage bound (\texttt{Conformal-bounded}, $1-\alpha$) and is \textbf{never} stamped \texttt{Deterministic}.
  \item \textbf{Proposer vs.\ authority in speculative decoding (\emph{The drafter proposes; the target decides})}: In byte-level and BPE token-level speculative decoding (Claims LA-5, LA-6, LA-7), \texttt{PagedBenesLUT6} acts strictly as a zero-FLOP proposer. Every emitted token is verified against the exact greedy distribution of the target model (\texttt{Mamba3CodeLM}, \texttt{LFM2.5-350M}, or \texttt{Qwen3.5-0.8B-Base}), guaranteeing \texttt{100.0\%} lossless output parity.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{0.4 How to read this paper}\label{how-to-read}

Section 1 defines all notation, acronyms, and measurement labels before use. Section 2 states and proves four mathematical results governing cascade amortization, Bene\v{s} multicast routing, joint-mode minterm synthesis, and conformal wake gating (all machine-checked in Lean \texttt{4.36.0}, \texttt{proofs/}). Section 3 describes the reference implementation across Python, Rust, Zig, Go, Julia, SystemVerilog (\texttt{xck26}), \texttt{AirMoL} / \texttt{Expert-AirMoL} NVMe streaming, and \texttt{VerifiedCodeCascade}. Section 4 reports Claims \texttt{LA-1} through \texttt{LA-23} with explicit measurement columns. Section 5 compares prior work. Section 6 records threats to validity, non-claims, and executable falsifiers \texttt{LA-F1} through \texttt{LA-F23}. Appendix A lists exact reproduction commands.

\section{1. Notation, terms, and measurement labels}\label{notation}

All symbols, hardware primitives, model acronyms, and evidence labels used in later sections are collected below.

\addlinespace[3pt]

\texttt{PLD} / \texttt{REST} & Prompt Lookup Decoding (Saxena, 2023) and Retrieval-Based Speculative Decoding (He et al., 2024), zero-FLOP retrieval drafters evaluated as baselines. \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\texttt{Medusa} / \texttt{EAGLE-2} & Parallel MLP heads (Cai et al., 2024) and context-aware dynamic draft trees (Li et al., 2024), neural speculative drafters evaluated as baselines. \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\texttt{Deterministic} / \texttt{Check-safe} & Provenance of an output verified by an exact specification check (\texttt{RCE}); distinct from statistical model predictions. \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\texttt{Conformal-bounded} & Provenance of a \texttt{LUT6} page crystallized under the oracle-free \texttt{JEPA-EBRM} ACI gate ($1-\alpha$ marginal coverage); never promoted to \texttt{Deterministic}. \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\texttt{ModelGenerated} & Provenance of a neural model proposal prior to verification. \texttt{ModelGenerated $\nrightarrow$ Deterministic}. \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textbf{Measured (software / I/O)} & Exact byte counts transferred via OS syscalls (\texttt{os.pread} with \texttt{F\_NOCACHE}), resident memory allocations, wall-clock seconds on Apple M5 Max, and exact bit/token agreement. Not joules. \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textbf{Simulated (RTL)} & Gate/BRAM/DSP counts from Yosys \texttt{0.71+} (\texttt{synth\_xilinx -family xcup}) and cycle counts (\texttt{CPI = 1}) from Verilator \texttt{5.054}. Not physical board timing closure or rail power (\texttt{board\_metered = false}). \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textbf{Estimated (\texttt{est\_j})} & Analytical joules computed from operation counts or TDP-time stand-ins (\texttt{joule\_meter.py} fallback when \texttt{RAPL}/\texttt{NVML} hardware counters are absent on macOS). Comparable only across arms inside the same formula; never silicon watts. \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textbf{Lean \texttt{4.36.0}} (\texttt{proofs/}) & Interactive theorem prover and dependently typed kernel (Moura and Ullrich, 2021) verifying all \texttt{27} theorems across \texttt{LutAdapt/\{Cascade,BenesCopy,JointMinterm,ConformalProvenance\}.lean} (\texttt{0} \texttt{sorry}, \texttt{0} custom axioms). \\
\addlinespace[3pt]
\textbf{\texttt{Nemotron-3-120B-A12B} \& \texttt{Devstral-2-24B}} & NVIDIA's \texttt{120B} (\texttt{12B} active) Hybrid Mamba-Attention Sparse-MoE checkpoint (\texttt{Q3\_K\_S}, \texttt{58.1 GB} RSS) and Mistral's \texttt{24B} dense SWE checkpoint (\texttt{Q4\_K\_M}, \texttt{16.8 GB} RSS), served via Metal \texttt{llama-server}. \\

\end{longtable}

\section{2. Mathematical formulations and four results}\label{math-results}

\subsection{2.1 Result 1: Cascade ordering with amortized downward compilation}\label{result-1}

Consider gears $i \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\}$ tried sequentially on an input $x$. Gear $i$ closes the task ($C(z)=1$) with probability $p_i$ at expected analytical energy $c_i > 0$. By Simon and Kadane (1975) (derived for Mixture of Limits in \emph{Metered Commit Gates}, Section II.2 [same house]), the expected energy of a permutation $\sigma$ under independent close events is
\[
  \mathbb{E}[J_\sigma] = \sum_{k=1}^{n} c_{\sigma(k)} \prod_{j < k} \bigl(1 - p_{\sigma(j)}\bigr),
\]
which is minimized if and only if gears are ordered by non-increasing ratio $p_{\sigma(1)}/c_{\sigma(1)} \ge \dots \ge p_{\sigma(n)}/c_{\sigma(n)}$.

Now suppose a stationary regime $\mathcal{D}_r$ persists for a remaining horizon of $\Delta T$ queries. Initially Rung 0 (\texttt{LUT6}) has coverage $p_0 = 0$ on $\mathcal{D}_r$, so every query escalates to a wake path of expected cost $c_{\text{wake}}$ (for example Rung 3 at cost $c_3$). After collecting $N_{\text{wake}}$ certified wake pairs $(x, y^*)$, \texttt{SleepCrystallizer} expends one-time compilation energy $E_{\text{compile}}$ to synthesize a Rung 0 \texttt{LUT6} page with certified coverage $p_0' \in (0, 1]$ and per-query lookup cost $c_0 \ll c_{\text{wake}}$.

\textbf{Proposition 1 (Net energy condition for downward crystallization).} \emph{Assuming the wake path closes the task ($p_{\text{wake}} = 1$), downward compilation strictly reduces expected cumulative spend over the remaining stationary horizon $\Delta T$ if and only if}
\[
  \Delta T \cdot \bigl(p_0' c_{\text{wake}} - c_0\bigr) > E_{\text{compile}}, \qquad \text{and} \qquad \frac{p_0'}{c_0} > \frac{1}{c_{\text{wake}}}.
\]
\emph{Proof.} Without crystallization, the remaining $\Delta T$ queries each incur expected energy $c_{\text{wake}}$, totaling $\Delta T \cdot c_{\text{wake}}$. With crystallization at one-time cost $E_{\text{compile}}$, Rung 0 precedes the wake path whenever $p_0'/c_0 > 1/c_{\text{wake}} \iff c_0 < p_0' c_{\text{wake}}$. Each subsequent query tries Rung 0 at cost $c_0$ and escalates with probability $1 - p_0'$, incurring expected energy $c_0 + (1 - p_0') c_{\text{wake}}$ and saving $p_0' c_{\text{wake}} - c_0 > 0$ per query. Summing over $\Delta T$ steps yields net cumulative savings $\Delta T(p_0' c_{\text{wake}} - c_0) - E_{\text{compile}} > 0$. When $c_0 / c_{\text{wake}} < 10^{-4}$, the threshold simplifies to $\Delta T \cdot p_0' c_{\text{wake}} > E_{\text{compile}}$. $\blacksquare$

\textbf{Consequence on non-stationary drift.} If regimes churn faster than $\Delta T^* = E_{\text{compile}} / (p_0' c_{\text{wake}} - c_0)$, compiling \texttt{LUT6} pages loses energy. \texttt{SleepCrystallizer} therefore gates compilation behind a minimum certified buffer $N_{\text{wake}}$ and holdout agreement $\tau_{\text{agree}} = 0.985$.

\subsection{2.2 Result 2: Non-blocking \texorpdfstring{$512 \times 512$}{512x512} Bene\v{s}--Copy--Bene\v{s} multicast routing}\label{result-2}

A Rung 0 layer of $G = 80$ dynamic \texttt{LUT6} primitives requires $M = 6G = 480$ input pins drawn from an $N = 512$-bit input bus. Because learned Boolean features exhibit variable fan-out (one input wire may feed zero \texttt{LUT6} pins or dozens), a standard $N \times N$ permutation Bene\v{s} network cannot route multicast assignments, while a flat $M \times N$ multiplexer crossbar requires $480$ independent $512:1$ muxes (\texttt{119,478} CLB LUTs already at $384$ ports, overflowing the \texttt{117,120}-LUT Kria KV260 \texttt{xck26}; Claim LA-3).

\textbf{Theorem 2 (Contention-free multicast via Bene\v{s}--Copy--Bene\v{s}).} \emph{Let $N = 2^n$ ($n=9, N=512$) and $M \le N$ ($M=480$). Any arbitrary many-to-one multicast mapping $\phi: \{0, \dots, M-1\} \to \{0, \dots, N-1\}$ is realizable without wire contention in a three-cascade $2 \times 2$ switch fabric $\mathcal{B}_1 \to \mathcal{C} \to \mathcal{B}_2$ of depth}
\[
  D(N) = 2(2\log_2 N - 1) + \log_2 N = 5\log_2 N - 2 = 43 \text{ stages}
\]
\emph{comprising $43 \times (N/2) = 11{,}008$ SRAM-configured $2 \times 2$ cells, with $O(N \log N)$ configuration runtime and zero FPGA place-and-route.}

\emph{Proof.} Let $U = \{s_1, \dots, s_r\}$ be the $r \le M$ distinct active sources in the image of $\phi$ with fan-out multiplicities $m_1, \dots, m_r \ge 1$ summing to $\sum_{j=1}^r m_j = M \le N$. Define partial sums $S_0 = 0$ and $S_j = \sum_{i=1}^j m_i$.
(1) \emph{Concentration ($\mathcal{B}_1$, $2n-1 = 17$ stages)}: Choose any bijection $\pi_1$ on $\{0,\dots,N-1\}$ mapping $\pi_1(s_j) = S_{j-1}$ for $j=1,\dots,r$. By the Bene\v{s}--Waksman theorem (Bene\v{s}, 1965; Waksman, 1968), $\pi_1$ decomposes into $2n-1$ stages of $N/2$ $2\times 2$ switches via $O(N \log N)$ bipartite 2-coloring.
(2) \emph{Contiguous fan-out ($\mathcal{C}$, $n = 9$ stages)}: Replicating each seed at $S_{j-1}$ across the contiguous interval $[S_{j-1}, S_j - 1]$ defines disjoint monotonic intervals $[S_0, S_1-1], \dots, [S_{r-1}, S_r-1]$. By Thompson's monotonic copy network theorem (Thompson, 1978; Lee, 1988), binary-tree interval splitting across $n$ butterfly stages preserves strict endpoint monotonicity at every stage and routes all $M$ copies with zero link contention.
(3) \emph{Output permutation ($\mathcal{B}_2$, $2n-1 = 17$ stages)}: Map each contiguous block $[S_{j-1}, S_j-1]$ bijectively onto the target pin set $\phi^{-1}(s_j) \subset \{0,\dots,M-1\}$ and pad $[M, N-1]$ arbitrarily to form a bijection $\pi_2$, routed by Waksman's algorithm on $\mathcal{B}_2$. Composing $\pi_2 \circ \mathcal{C} \circ \pi_1$ delivers $\phi(q)$ to every pin $q \in \{0,\dots,M-1\}$. $\blacksquare$

\subsection{2.3 Result 3: Joint-mode minterm synthesis vs.\ marginal coordinate argmax}\label{result-3}

Standard differentiable LUT layers (Petersen et al., 2022) predict multi-bit or multi-token outputs $\mathbf{Y} = (Y_1, \dots, Y_B) \in \mathcal{Y}$ via independent binary gates $\hat{y}_b(x) = \mathbb{I}[\Pr(Y_b = 1 \mid \psi(x)) > 1/2]$ on each bit-plane $b \in \{1,\dots,B\}$. On multi-modal conditional distributions --- such as code after \texttt{if (} vs.\ \texttt{return }, or $K$-token BPE bursts --- independent bit-plane rounding creates invalid \textbf{chimera} tokens.

\textbf{Proposition 3 (Dominance and zero-chimera property of joint-mode minterms).} \emph{For any discrete context map $\psi: \mathcal{X} \to \mathcal{C}$ and discrete burst space $\mathcal{Y}$ encoded into $B \le 64$ bits, define the joint-mode minterm table $T^*(c) = \arg\max_{\mathbf{y} \in \mathcal{Y}} \Pr(\mathbf{Y} = \mathbf{y} \mid \psi(X) = c)$ and the marginal bit-plane predictor $\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\text{marg}}(c) = (\arg\max_{v \in \{0,1\}} \Pr(Y_b = v \mid \psi(X)=c))_{b=1}^B$. Then:}
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \emph{$\Pr[\mathbf{Y} = T^*(\psi(X))] \ge \Pr[\mathbf{Y} = \hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\text{marg}}(\psi(X))]$, with strict inequality whenever marginal bit majorities disagree with the joint mode.}
  \item \emph{Every output of $T^*(c)$ belongs to $\operatorname{supp}(\mathbf{Y} \mid \psi(X)=c)$ (zero chimera bursts), whereas $\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\text{marg}}(c)$ can achieve \texttt{0\%} exact-burst accuracy.}
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Proof.} Pointwise for each context $c$, $\Pr(\mathbf{Y} = g(c) \mid c) \le \max_{\mathbf{y} \in \mathcal{Y}} \Pr(\mathbf{Y} = \mathbf{y} \mid c) = \Pr(\mathbf{Y} = T^*(c) \mid c)$ for every deterministic $g(c)$; taking expectations gives (1), and positive probability mass at the mode gives $T^*(c) \in \operatorname{supp}(\mathbf{Y}\mid c)$. For the counterexample in (2), let $B=3$ and place conditional mass $0.40, 0.35, 0.25$ on $(1,1,0), (1,0,1), (0,1,1)$: every marginal bit satisfies $\Pr(Y_b=1\mid c) \ge 0.60 > 1/2$, so $\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\text{marg}}(c) = (1,1,1) \notin \operatorname{supp}(\mathbf{Y}\mid c)$ (\texttt{0\%} exact match vs.\ \texttt{40\%} for $T^*(c)=(1,1,0)$). $\blacksquare$

\textbf{Universal 64-bit BPE token packing.} For any pretrained tokenizer of vocabulary size $|V|$, each token requires $b_V = \lceil \log_2 |V| \rceil$ bits, packing $K = \lfloor 64 / b_V \rfloor$ tokens losslessly into one \texttt{uint64} word: \texttt{LFM2.5-350M} ($|V|=65{,}536, b_V=16 \implies K=4$, \texttt{64} bits) and \texttt{Qwen3.5-0.8B-Base} ($|V|=248{,}320, b_V=18 \implies K=3$, \texttt{54} bits $\le 64$).

\subsection{2.4 Result 4: Oracle-free latent \texttt{JEPA-EBRM} Split-Conformal + Online ACI gate}\label{result-4}

In open-world perceptual or telemetry tasks without a deterministic \texttt{RCE} check, gating \texttt{SleepCrystallizer} by softmax Maximum Softmax Probability (MSP) or logit energy (Liu et al., 2020) suffers from high-confidence Extrapolation far from training data (Hein et al., 2019), crystallizing hallucinated labels into \texttt{LUT6} banks (\texttt{17.6\%--24.3\%} false risk in Claim LA-4). We define a quadratically regularized latent \texttt{JEPA-EBRM} nonconformity score over encoder $z = f_\theta(x) \in \mathbb{R}^d$ and class prototypes $\{\mu_y\}$:
\[
  E_\phi(x, y) = \frac{1}{2}\|f_\theta(x) - \mu_y\|_2^2 + \frac{\lambda_r}{2}\|f_\theta(x)\|_2^2 + \psi_\phi(f_\theta(x), y), \qquad \lambda_r > 0, \quad |\psi_\phi(z,y)| \le C_\psi.
\]
Calibration on $n$ exchangeable held-out pairs sets $\hat{Q}_{1-\alpha} = \operatorname{Quantile}_{\lceil (n+1)(1-\alpha) \rceil / n}(\{E_\phi(X_i, Y_i)\}_{i=1}^n)$. Online, Adaptive Conformal Inference (ACI; Gibbs and Cand\`es, 2021) updates $\alpha_{t+1} = \alpha_t + \gamma(\alpha - \text{err}_t)$ and forms $\mathcal{C}_t(x) = \{y : E_\phi(x, y) \le \hat{Q}_{1-\alpha_t}\}$. \texttt{SleepCrystallizer} compiles $(x, \hat{y})$ if and only if $|\mathcal{C}_t(x)| = 1$, stamping provenance \texttt{Conformal-bounded} (\texttt{ModelGenerated $\nrightarrow$ Deterministic}).

\textbf{Proposition 4 (Coverage, adaptive shift recovery, and radial OOD abstention).}
\emph{(1) Under exchangeable $(X_{\text{test}}, Y_{\text{test}})$ at fixed $\alpha_t=\alpha$, $\Pr[Y_{\text{test}} \in \mathcal{C}(X_{\text{test}})] \ge 1 - \alpha$ (Vovk et al., 2005; Lei et al., 2018). (2) Under arbitrary bounded shift with $\alpha_t \in (0,1)$, ACI satisfies $\lim_{T\to\infty} T^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^T \text{err}_t = \alpha$ (Gibbs and Cand\`es, 2021). (3) Because $|\psi_\phi| \le C_\psi$ and $\lambda_r > 0$, $\min_y E_\phi(x,y) \ge \frac{\lambda_r}{2}\|z\|_2^2 - C_\psi \to +\infty$ as $\|z\|_2 \to \infty$, so every ray leaving radius $R(\alpha_t) = \sqrt{2(\hat{Q}_{1-\alpha_t} + C_\psi)/\lambda_r}$ yields $\mathcal{C}_t(x) = \emptyset$ (explicit \texttt{Refuse} / abstain).} $\blacksquare$

\section{3. Architecture, polyglot reference suite, and KV260 RTL}\label{architecture}

\subsection{3.1 The four-rung downward-compilation cascade}\label{cascade-arch}

Figure~\ref{fig:cascade} shows the closed-loop execution and downward-compilation dataflow implemented in \texttt{src/clai/}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/mol-downward-cascade.png}
  \caption{Downward compilation across a four-rung Mixture of Limits cascade. Design diagram, not a board power measurement. Receipts keep analytical \texttt{est\_j} separate from \texttt{measured\_j = None}.}
  \label{fig:cascade}
\end{figure}

Every incoming input enters \textbf{Rung 0} (\texttt{difflut\_net.py}, \texttt{code\_corpus\_speculative.py}). On a certified cache hit, Rung 0 closes immediately with zero floating-point operations. On a miss or ambiguity: \textbf{Rung 1} (\texttt{Mamba-3} Complex MIMO SSD, \texttt{triton\_mamba3\_ssd.py}) updates its constant-size complex recurrent state; \textbf{Rung 2} (\texttt{HC-SOINN}, \texttt{hc\_soinn.py}) tracks topological prototypes and isolates novel regimes; \textbf{Rung 3} (\texttt{JEPA-EBRM} and \texttt{AirMoL}) performs latent energy verification or streams 4-bit foundation model shards from NVMe; and \textbf{\texttt{SleepCrystallizer}} (\texttt{sleep\_crystallizer.py}) warm-starts Boolean truth tables from Walsh--Hadamard correlations, computes Waksman Bene\v{s} switch schedules (\texttt{benes\_lut\_fabric.py}), verifies holdout agreement $\ge 98.5\%$, and atomically flips the active \texttt{LUT6} bank pointer.

\subsection{3.2 Polyglot systems suite}\label{polyglot-arch}

Five independent implementations in \texttt{src/clai/} and \texttt{sys/} cross-check the cascade across runtimes:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Python 3.12 + PyTorch + Custom Triton Kernel (\texttt{src/clai/})}: Reference 4-rung engine, Triton \texttt{LUT6} kernel (\texttt{triton\_lut6.py}), Split-Conformal ACI governance, SystemVerilog emitter, and \texttt{AirMoL} NVMe streamer.
  \item \textbf{Rust (\texttt{sys/rust/}, \texttt{Burn 0.22.1} + \texttt{CubeCL} Metal GPU + \texttt{Rayon})}: Full 4-rung Metal GPU training and sleep crystallization on Apple M5 Max, emitting standalone KV260 Verilog (\texttt{artifacts/rust\_clai\_rung0\_kv260.v}).
  \item \textbf{Zig \texttt{0.17.1} (\texttt{sys/zig/src/mol\_baremetal.zig})}: Bare-metal \texttt{std.Io.Threaded} cascade using \texttt{@Vector(8, f32)} SIMD and \texttt{@popCount} with strictly \texttt{0} heap bytes allocated in the hot inference loop.
  \item \textbf{Go \texttt{1.27.4} (\texttt{sys/go/mol\_dataplane.go})}: Concurrent worker-pool L7 packet dataplane (\texttt{bits.OnesCount64}) verified at \texttt{0 B/op} and \texttt{0 allocs/op}.
  \item \textbf{Julia \texttt{1.13.4} (\texttt{sys/julia/mol\_scientific.jl})}: Reverse-mode automatic differentiation through smooth Walsh--Hadamard \texttt{DiffLUT6} polynomials via \texttt{Enzyme.jl} (\texttt{6.11e-10} max finite-difference discrepancy).
\end{itemize}

\subsection{3.3 Kria KV260 (\texttt{xck26}) SystemVerilog RTL and \texttt{RAMB36E2} page cache}\label{rtl-arch}

Figure~\ref{fig:benes} shows the hardware datapath in \texttt{hw/kv260/rtl/} (\texttt{clai\_kv260\_axi\_top.sv}, \texttt{clai\_benes\_hotswap\_core.sv}, and \texttt{clai\_benes\_hotswap\_core.sv}).

\begin{figure}[htbp]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/benes-paged-silicon.png}
  \caption{$512\times 512$ Bene\v{s}--Copy--Bene\v{s} multicast fabric and $81 \times \texttt{RAMB36E2}$ single-cycle \texttt{LUT6} page cache targeting AMD Kria KV260 (\texttt{xck26}). Synthesized in Yosys \texttt{0.71+} (\texttt{synth\_xilinx -family xcup}) and cycle-simulated in Verilator \texttt{5.054}; \texttt{board\_metered = false}.}
  \label{fig:benes}
\end{figure}

Reloading a single shadow \texttt{LUT6} bank (\texttt{5,120} bits) over 64-bit AXI4-Lite stalls switching for \texttt{82} cycles (\texttt{328 ns} at \texttt{250 MHz}), whereas storing \texttt{512} pages in distributed flip-flops explodes to \texttt{926,619} CLB LUTs (\texttt{791\%} of \texttt{xck26}). \texttt{clai\_benes\_hotswap\_core.sv} maps \texttt{512} resident pages (\texttt{5,120} \texttt{INIT} bits + \texttt{48}-bit context tag + \texttt{1} valid bit, aligned to $81 \times 64 = 5{,}184$ bits/page) across \textbf{\texttt{81} parallel \texttt{RAMB36E2} blocks} in $512 \times 64$ simple dual-port mode: Port A services asynchronous AXI4-Lite fills from the ARM PS while Port B performs a 9-bit XOR-fold hash lookup, reads all \texttt{5,120} \texttt{INIT} bits + \texttt{48}-bit tag in \textbf{\texttt{1} clock cycle (\texttt{4.0 ns} at \texttt{250 MHz} nominal, \texttt{CPI = 1.0})}, checks \texttt{tag\_hit}, and evaluates the \texttt{80} Bene\v{s}-routed \texttt{LUT6}s in a single beat.

\subsection{3.4 \texttt{AirMoL}: Zero-extra-sweep 4-bit NVMe layer streaming on 2026 hybrid models}\label{airmol-arch}

Figure~\ref{fig:airmol} illustrates \texttt{AirMoL} (\texttt{src/clai/frontier/air\_mol\_streamer.py} and \texttt{hf\_air\_mol\_pretrained.py}). Standard \texttt{AirLLM}-style layer streaming sweeps the entire model from NVMe once per generated token (\texttt{T=1}) and spills Transformer KV-caches to flash.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/airmol-nvme-speculation.png}
  \caption{\texttt{AirMoL} zero-extra-sweep 4-bit NVMe layer streaming with universal $\le 64$-bit joint-mode token \texttt{LUT6} speculation on \texttt{LiquidAI/LFM2.5-350M} and \texttt{Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B-Base}. NVMe bytes, peak active RAM, exact token agreement, and wall-clock throughput are measured on Apple M5 Max; joules are unmetered.}
  \label{fig:airmol}
\end{figure}

\texttt{AirMoL} combines four mechanisms: (1) group-wise 4-bit (\texttt{INT4}, group size \texttt{128}) NVMe layer shards read via POSIX \texttt{os.pread} under Darwin \texttt{fcntl(fd, F\_NOCACHE, 1)} so OS page caching cannot hide flash I/O; (2) \texttt{TokenPagedBenesLUT6Drafter} proposing $K \in \{3, 4\}$ BPE tokens packed in one \texttt{uint64} at \texttt{0} FLOPs and \texttt{0} NVMe reads; (3) single-sweep batch verification of $[t_0, d_1, \dots, d_K]$ through each streamed layer shard once; and (4) \textbf{in-RAM hybrid state rollback}, snapshotting compact recurrent/window states (\texttt{ShortConv}+\texttt{SlidingAttn} in Oct 2026 \texttt{LFM3-1.7B}; \texttt{Short-Conv} in \texttt{LFM2.5}; \texttt{Gated DeltaNet} in \texttt{Qwen3.5}/\texttt{Qwen3.6}) across the $K+1$ positions inside each layer and truncating the sparse \texttt{GQA} KV-cache to \texttt{seq\_len + m + 1} upon partial draft mismatch --- incurring \textbf{zero extra NVMe sweeps}.

\subsection{3.5 \texttt{Expert-AirMoL} Sparse-MoE NVMe streaming, \texttt{Sub1Bit-LUT6}, and \texttt{VerifiedCodeCascade}}\label{moe-arch}

Scaling disk-offloaded inference to frontier \texttt{8.47B} and \texttt{34.66B} sparse Mixture-of-Experts (\texttt{MoE}) architectures exposes a second \texttt{AirLLM} bottleneck: dense layer streaming reads all $E$ experts per layer (\texttt{32} in \texttt{LFM2.5-8B-A1B}, \texttt{256} in \texttt{Qwen3.6-35B-A3B}) even though the router activates only $k \ll E$ experts per token (\texttt{4/32} and \texttt{8/256}).

\begin{figure}[htbp]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/moe-expert-cascade.png}
  \caption{\texttt{Expert-AirMoL} router-sparse 4-bit expert-indexed NVMe streaming combined with \texttt{BenesLUT6} burst speculation and the multi-rung $C(z)$-verified code synthesis and crystallization loop (\texttt{moe\_air\_mol.py}, \texttt{verified\_code\_cascade.py}).}
  \label{fig:moe}
\end{figure}

\texttt{Expert-AirMoL} splits each MoE layer into a compact non-expert header shard and a fixed-stride \texttt{INT4} block-64 \texttt{.experts.pack4} NVMe file, issuing \texttt{F\_NOCACHE} \texttt{os.pread} calls exclusively for the active union $\mathcal{U}_l = \bigcup_{t=0}^{K} \operatorname{TopK}(h_{l,t})$ across the $K+1$ burst positions (\texttt{6.90$\times$} and \texttt{12.30$\times$} NVMe I/O reductions; Claim LA-9). In parallel, \texttt{Sub1BitLUT6FactorLinear} (\texttt{0.576 bpw}, rank $r=258=43\times 6$) replaces the dense runtime binary matrix multiplications of \texttt{LittleBit-1 / LittleBit-2} (Lee et al., 2025; Lee and Kim, 2026) with \texttt{6}-input \texttt{LUT6} partial-sum tables (\texttt{0} binary MACs; Claim LA-10), while \texttt{VerifiedCodeCascade} gates commits across \texttt{LFM3-1.7B} $\to$ \texttt{Gemma-4-E2B-it} $\to$ \texttt{LFM2.5-8B-A1B} $\to$ \texttt{Qwen3.6-35B-A3B} via a \texttt{0.35s}-bounded contract verifier $C(z)\in\{0,1\}$ and compiles verified AST skills into Rungs \texttt{0--1} (Claim LA-11).

\subsection{3.6 The nine-rung capability ratchet and the Compilation Threshold \texorpdfstring{($\Delta T > \Delta T^*$)}{(Delta T > Delta T*)}}\label{avalanche-threshold}

Across the progression of computer intelligence (companion study: \emph{The Communication Tool Avalanche}, \url{https://research.openie.dev/papers/communication-avalanche/} \texttt{[same house]}), capability rungs advance as a ratchet divided at Rung 5 by Proposition 1's Compilation Threshold (Figure~\ref{fig:avalanche}): Rungs 1--5 (\emph{Search}, \emph{Generation}, \emph{Reasoning}, \emph{Communication}, and \emph{Tool Use}) stall under ephemeral cold-start \texttt{MAC} inference ($\Delta T \approx 1 \le \Delta T^*$, \texttt{56.25\%--95.83\%} standalone broken patches), whereas \textbf{Rung 6 (\emph{Immersion})} extends stationary persistence past $\Delta T^* = E_{\text{compile}}/(p_0' c_{\text{wake}} - c_0)$ under $C(z)=1$ (\texttt{0.00\%} broken commits), unlocking \textbf{Rung 7 (\emph{Competence}, $p_0'\to 1$ at \texttt{62.4 pJ} \texttt{est\_j} / \texttt{272 $\mu$J} \texttt{reported\_j})}, \textbf{Rung 8 (\emph{Ecology})}, and \textbf{Rung 9 (\emph{Economy})}.

\begin{figure}[htbp]
  \centering
  \includegraphics[width=\linewidth]{figures/avalanche-compilation-threshold.png}
  \caption{The nine-rung capability ratchet and Proposition 1's Compilation Threshold ($\Delta T > \Delta T^*$), separating \texttt{reported\_j} (Apple M5 Max) and \texttt{est\_j} (\texttt{AMD DS925 VCD}) across twelve orders of magnitude (\texttt{measured\_j = None}, \texttt{board\_metered = false}).}
  \label{fig:avalanche}
\end{figure}

\section{4. Claims and results}\label{claims-results}

Every claim below corresponds to an executable script and persisted artifact in \texttt{artifacts/}. Measurement labels follow Section 1 strictly.

\subsection{4.1 Claim LA-1: Analytical \texttt{J/VC} collapse on synthetic tasks and real \texttt{.pcap} traffic}\label{claim-la1}

\texttt{scripts/run\_collapse\_curve.py} (5,120 synthetic piecewise-stationary episodes) and \texttt{scripts/run\_realworld\_domain.py} (5,120 real L7 enterprise packets in \texttt{artifacts/enterprise\_drift\_capture.pcap}, with an unannounced IEEE \texttt{802.1Q} VLAN \texttt{0x8100} shift at packet 2,500) evaluate downward \texttt{LUT6} compilation under deterministic \texttt{RCE} holdout verification ($\ge 98.5\%$).

{\footnotesize\setlength{\tabcolsep}{4pt}
\begin{longtable}{@{}L{0.25\linewidth}C{0.09\linewidth}C{0.11\linewidth}C{0.15\linewidth}C{0.10\linewidth}L{0.16\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{Workload \& Window} & \textbf{R0 Share} & \textbf{Pass Rate} & \textbf{Analytical \texttt{est\_j/VC}} & \textbf{RTL Parity} & \textbf{Measurement} \\
\midrule
\endhead
Synthetic ep.\ \texttt{1--512} (cold) & \texttt{0.00\%} & \texttt{0.0\%$\to$52.0\%} & \texttt{2,323.18 est\_j} & --- & SW + \texttt{est\_j} \\
Synthetic ep.\ \texttt{3,073--3,584} & \texttt{52.73\%} & \texttt{60.55\%} & \texttt{2.59 est\_j} (\texttt{895$\times$}) & \texttt{100.0\%} & Verilator + \texttt{est\_j} \\
\texttt{.pcap} pkts \texttt{1--512} (cold IPv4) & \texttt{0.00\%} & \texttt{73.05\%} & \texttt{3.3351 est\_j} & \texttt{100.0\%} & Verilator + \texttt{est\_j} \\
\texttt{.pcap} pkts \texttt{1,537--2,048} (cryst.) & \texttt{100.00\%} & \texttt{100.00\%} & \texttt{0.000140 est\_j} & \texttt{100.0\%} & Verilator + \texttt{est\_j} \\
\texttt{.pcap} pkts \texttt{2,049--2,560} (VLAN drift) & \texttt{2.73\%} & \texttt{68.55\%} & \texttt{3.0485 est\_j} & \texttt{100.0\%} & Breaker + \texttt{est\_j} \\
\textbf{\texttt{.pcap} pkts \texttt{2,561--3,072} (re-cryst.)} & \textbf{\texttt{100.00\%}} & \textbf{\texttt{100.00\%}} & \textbf{\texttt{0.000131 est\_j}} & \textbf{\texttt{100.0\%}} & \textbf{Verilator + \texttt{est\_j}} \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
}

At packet \texttt{2,500}, the circuit breaker catches the 4-byte VLAN header offset (\texttt{96.9\%} mismatch against the pre-drift \texttt{LUT6} bank), flushes Rung 0, wakes Rung 1/3, and re-crystallizes a new \texttt{LUT6} bank within one sleep window (\texttt{5,120/5,120} exact Verilator RTL parity). The \texttt{25,458$\times$} ratio compares \texttt{est\_j} analytical counts inside the same model, not metered board power.

\begin{longtable}{@{}L{0.29\linewidth}C{0.06\linewidth}C{0.12\linewidth}C{0.16\linewidth}C{0.09\linewidth}C{0.08\linewidth}L{0.10\linewidth}@{}}
\toprule
\textbf{RTL Architecture} & \textbf{Pages} & \textbf{Switch} & \textbf{CLB LUTs (\texttt{xck26})} & \textbf{RAMB36} & \textbf{Drift Acc.} & \textbf{Evidence} \\
\midrule
\endhead
\texttt{2A} Flat \texttt{512:1} Crossbar (\texttt{64} LUT6) & \texttt{1} & \texttt{82 cyc} & \texttt{119,478} (\textbf{102.0\%!}) & \texttt{0} & \texttt{98.44\%} & Yosys sim \\
\texttt{2B} Compact Crossbar (\texttt{32} LUT6) & \texttt{1} & \texttt{82 cyc} & \texttt{59,739} (\texttt{51.0\%}) & \texttt{0} & \texttt{95.94\%} & Yosys sim \\
\texttt{2C} Fixed-Wire DiffLogic / WNN & \texttt{1} & Re-PAR & \texttt{160} (\texttt{0.14\%}) & \texttt{0} & \textbf{\texttt{52.34\%}} & Yosys sim \\
\texttt{2D} Single-Page Bene\v{s}--Copy--Bene\v{s} & \texttt{1} & \texttt{82 cyc} & \texttt{40,155} (\texttt{34.3\%}) & \texttt{0} & \texttt{98.44\%} & Yosys+Veril. \\
\texttt{P2} Distributed FF + \texttt{512:1} Mux & \texttt{512} & \texttt{1 cyc} & \texttt{926,619} (\textbf{791.2\%!}) & \texttt{0} & \texttt{98.44\%} & Simulated \\
\textbf{\texttt{P3} Bene\v{s} + \texttt{81} \texttt{RAMB36E2} (Ours)} & \textbf{\texttt{512}} & \textbf{\texttt{1 cyc (4ns)}} & \textbf{\texttt{41,186 (35.2\%)}} & \textbf{\texttt{81 (56\%)}} & \textbf{\texttt{98.44\%}} & \textbf{Yosys+Veril.} \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}
}

All six configurations synthesize with \textbf{\texttt{0} \texttt{DSP48} slices}. Adding \texttt{512} resident \texttt{LUT6} context pages via \texttt{81} \texttt{RAMB36E2} blocks adds only \texttt{+1,031} CLB LUTs (\texttt{+0.88\%} of \texttt{xck26}) over the single-page Bene\v{s} core while accelerating page switching \texttt{82$\times$} (\texttt{CPI = 1.0} across \texttt{24/24} Verilator assertions).

On October 2026 \texttt{LiquidAI/LFM3-1.7B}, active weight RAM stays capped at \textbf{\texttt{250.0 MB}} (\texttt{3.79\%} of \texttt{6,594.85 MB} FP32) while post-wake \texttt{AirMoL} cuts NVMe reads by \textbf{\texttt{2.74$\times$}} (\texttt{25.045} $\to$ \texttt{9.131 GB}) and reaches \textbf{\texttt{1.32 tok/s} (\texttt{2.49$\times$} speedup)}. On CPU-bound \texttt{Qwen3.5-0.8B}, cold batch verification lowers \texttt{tok/s} (\texttt{0.32} vs.\ \texttt{0.53}) until wake crystallization lifts acceptance to \texttt{2.74 tok/sweep} (\texttt{1.11 tok/s}).

\subsection{4.27 Claim LA-27: Closed-form \texttt{DiffLUT-Net} (\texttt{arXiv:2609.09254}) \texttt{-64.06\%} pruning, wobble FWHT filter, \& Means Test}\label{claim-la27}

Closed-form 64-minterm multilinear relaxation with Straight-Through port lock-in (Ye et al., 2026) cuts active \texttt{LUT6} gates \textbf{\texttt{64.06\%}} (\texttt{64 $\to$ 23}, packing \textbf{\texttt{2.78$\times$} more skills} in RP2040 \texttt{264 KB} SRAM: \texttt{690} vs.\ \texttt{248} slots, and \texttt{992} for \texttt{16}-LUT wobble crystals). By Parseval's identity ($\sum_S \hat{w}(S)^2 = 4096$), 1-hot masks leak $7\times 4 = 28$ AC energy into $|S|\ge 5$ and collapse to \texttt{0.0\%} noisy hit (tripping the \texttt{< 50\%} kill criterion), whereas every 2-bit wobble \texttt{LUT6} (\texttt{0x0F << 4q}) has \textbf{exact \texttt{0}} degree-$\ge 5$ Walsh energy (\texttt{NucleusCodon.lean}), reaching \textbf{\texttt{95.0\%} noisy wake suppression, \texttt{0.0\%} silent corruption, and \texttt{100.0\%} OOD abstention}.

\section{6. Limits, threats to validity, not claimed, and falsifiers}\label{limits}

\textbf{Internal validity.} Downward crystallization pays off only when regime persistence $\Delta T$ exceeds Proposition 1's amortization threshold. On i.i.d.\ random bitstreams or per-step adversarial churn ($\Delta T = 1$), $p_0' \approx 0$ and the system falls back to \texttt{1.00 tok/sweep} plus Rung 0 lookup overhead. On \texttt{Qwen3.5-0.8B-Base} cold prompts (\texttt{Claim LA-7}), CPU verification of $K+1 = 4$ tokens reduced wall-clock speed (\texttt{0.32} vs.\ \texttt{0.53 tok/s}) despite cutting NVMe bytes (\texttt{8.581} vs.\ \texttt{12.674 GB}), showing that CPU batch verification requires post-wake draft acceptance (\texttt{2.74 tok/sweep} $\to$ \texttt{1.11 tok/s}) or GPU/FPGA offload to beat \texttt{T=1} latency.

\textbf{Construct validity.} \texttt{100.0\%} lossless parity guarantees bit-for-bit agreement with greedy target decoding; it does not raise the underlying capability ceiling of a \texttt{350M} or \texttt{0.8B} checkpoint. Likewise, \texttt{Conformal-bounded} ($1-\alpha$) bounds statistical miscoverage on the calibrated \texttt{JEPA-EBRM} manifold and never promotes neural proposals to \texttt{Deterministic}.

\textbf{External validity.} Foundation-model \texttt{AirMoL}, \texttt{Expert-AirMoL}, and coding benchmarks (\texttt{Claims LA-7, LA-9, LA-11, LA-15, LA-17, LA-20, LA-21}) were measured across eight open-weights checkpoints spanning \texttt{350M} to \textbf{\texttt{120B}} parameters (\texttt{LFM2.5-350M}, \texttt{Qwen3.5-0.8B-Base}, \texttt{LiquidAI/LFM3-1.7B}, \texttt{google/gemma-4-E2B-it}, \texttt{LiquidAI/LFM2.5-8B-A1B} \texttt{8.47B}, \texttt{Devstral-Small-2-24B}, \texttt{Qwen/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B} \texttt{34.66B}, and \textbf{\texttt{nvidia/Nemotron-3-Super-120B-A12B} \texttt{120B}}, plus \texttt{LittleBit-2} \texttt{0.55 bpw}) on Apple M5 Max (\texttt{128 GiB} Unified Memory), including \texttt{32} official \texttt{BigCodeBench v0.1.3} evaluations and \texttt{40} upstream \texttt{SWE-bench Verified} git-worktree evaluations across \texttt{django/django}, \texttt{pytest-dev/pytest}, \texttt{psf/requests}, and \texttt{sympy/sympy}. Unsharded cold-NVMe \texttt{70B} rows in \texttt{air\_mol\_nvme\_baselines.csv} remain labeled analytical baselines, while real \texttt{120B-A12B} execution is measured directly in Claim LA-21.

\textbf{Measurement validity.} Physical DC current-shunt \texttt{measured\_j} (\texttt{TI INA3221} \texttt{VDD\_CPU\_SOC\_MSS} and \texttt{INA238} \texttt{VIN} on \texttt{NVIDIA Jetson AGX Thor} solely in Claim LA-24, \texttt{board\_metered = true}) stays strictly separated from on-chip/OS \texttt{reported\_j} counters (\texttt{Linux RAPL}, \texttt{amdgpu PPT}, \texttt{NVML}, Darwin \texttt{L3 RUSAGE\_INFO\_V6} + \texttt{L2 IOReport GPU Energy}), whole-system macOS \texttt{PMU} deltas, and analytical/VCD \texttt{est\_j} estimates (Claim LA-22). Yosys \texttt{0.71+} and Verilator \texttt{5.054} verify structural \texttt{xck26} synthesis (\texttt{13} single-cycle logic levels and \texttt{170 <= 189} \texttt{IOB}s in \texttt{CE}-gated \texttt{P4-AXIS}) and \texttt{320/320} \texttt{CE} parity, not commercial Vivado post-PAR \texttt{250 MHz} timing closure or KV260 rail watts (\texttt{measured\_j = None} for all \texttt{xck26} RTL claims).

\textbf{Not claimed.} Not claimed here: physical KV260 bitstream loading or rail-metered joules; commercial Vivado post-PAR timing closure at \texttt{250 MHz}; silicon energy leadership over commercial ASICs; promotion of \texttt{ModelGenerated} outputs to \texttt{Deterministic}; or full \texttt{500}-instance \texttt{SWE-bench Verified} evaluation beyond the \texttt{40} real git-worktree evaluations across \texttt{django}, \texttt{pytest}, \texttt{requests}, and \texttt{sympy} in Claims LA-15 \& LA-20.

}
